B.4.
Simulations between query languages

Claim 4 (SPARQL to
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
).

The triple
(f_{12},g_{12},h_{21})
is a simulation of SPARQL in
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
.

Proof.

To prove this claim, we show that, for every SPARQL query
Q
and RDF graph
G
, it holds that
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}=h_{21}(\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}})
by induction on the structure of a normalized SPARQL query
Q
. In this proof, we assume that
\theta
is a
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
substitution for the variables of the
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
query
f_{12}(Q)
, and
\mu
is the SPARQL mapping
h_{21}(\theta)
. To show then that
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}=h_{21}(\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}})
, we have to prove that
\mu\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}
if and only if
\theta\in h_{21}(\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}})
, and
\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})=\operatorname{card}(\theta,h_{21}(\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}}))
.

(1)

Let
Q
be a triple pattern
({{?}{X}},p,{{?}{Y}})
. In this case, there is a corresponding version of the triple pattern as a
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
literal
\operatorname{triple}(X,p,Y)
, where
X
and
Y
are the corresponding variables for
{{?}{X}}
and
{{?}{Y}}
. The
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
query
f_{12}(Q)
is then
(q(X,Y),\Pi)
where
\Pi
is the program with a rule
q(X,Y)\leftarrow\operatorname{triple}(X,p,Y)
. Let
\theta
be the
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
substitution
\theta=(X/s,\,Y/o)
and
\mu
be the SPARQL mapping
h_{21}(\theta)=\{{{?}{X}}\mapsto s,\,{{?}{Y}}\mapsto o\}
.

(a)

According to the
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
semantics,
\theta\in\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}
if and only if
\operatorname{triple}(s,p,o)\in g_{12}(G)
. By the definition function
g_{12}
,
\operatorname{triple}(s,p,o)\in g_{12}(G)
if and only if
(s,p,o)\in G
. By the SPARQL semantics, the SPARQL mapping
\mu
is in
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}
if and only if
(s,p,o)\in G
. Hence,
\theta\in\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}
if and only if
\mu\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}
.

(b)

By construction,
\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})=1
and
\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})=1
. Hence,
\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})
.

We have shown that we can simulate triple patterns of the form
(X,p,Y)
with
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
queries. However, it is not difficult to apply the same argument for the other forms of triple patterns (e.g.,
(X,p,o)
or
(s,X,Y)
). Hence, SPARQL triple patterns are simulable with
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
.

(2)

Let
Q
be a query
(P_{1}\operatorname{AND}P_{2})
. Assume that
\operatorname{inScope}(P_{1})=\{{{?}{X}},{{?}{Y}}\}
and
\operatorname{inScope}(P_{2})=\{{{?}{X}},{{?}{Z}}\}
. The
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
query
f_{12}(Q)
is then
(q(X,Y,Z),\Pi)
where
\Pi
is the program that consists of the rules in the programs of queries
(p_{1}(X,Y),\Pi_{1})=f_{12}(P_{1})
and
(p_{2}(X,Z),\Pi_{2})=f_{12}(P_{2})
, the rule

\displaystyle q(X,Y,Z)\leftarrow p_{1}(X_{1},Y),p_{2}(X_{2},Z),\operatorname{comp}(X_{1},X_{2},X)

and the rules that define the compatibility between values (which may also included in
\Pi_{1}
and
\Pi_{2}
)

\displaystyle\operatorname{comp}(X,X,X)\leftarrow\operatorname{term}(X)

\displaystyle\operatorname{comp}(X,Y,X)\leftarrow\operatorname{term}(X),\operatorname{null}(Y)

\displaystyle\operatorname{comp}(Y,X,X)\leftarrow\operatorname{term}(X),\operatorname{null}(Y)

\displaystyle\operatorname{comp}(Y,Y,Y)\leftarrow\operatorname{null}(Y).

(a)

If
\theta\in\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}
then, by the semantics of
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
, there exists the
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
solutions
\theta_{1}=\{X_{1}/a_{1},Y/b\}
,
\theta_{2}=\{X_{2}/a_{2},Z/c\}
, and

\theta_{3}=\{X_{1}/a_{1},X_{2}/a_{2},X/a\}
such that

\displaystyle\{X_{1}/a_{1},Y/b\}\in\llbracket(p_{1}(X_{1},Y),\Pi_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)},

\displaystyle\{X_{2}/a_{2},Z/c\}\in\llbracket(p_{2}(X_{2},Z),\Pi_{2})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)},

\displaystyle\{X_{1}/a_{1},X_{2}/a_{2},X/a\}\in\llbracket(\operatorname{comp}(X_{1},X_{2},X),\Pi)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}.

By the induction hypothesis in
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
,
\theta_{1}\in\{X_{1}/a_{1},Y/b\}\in\llbracket(p_{1}(X_{1},Y),\Pi_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}
and
\theta_{2}\in\{X_{1}/a_{1},Y/b\}\in\llbracket(p_{2}(X_{1},Y),\Pi_{2})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}
if and only if mappings
\mu_{1}=h_{21}(\theta_{1})
and
\mu_{2}=h_{21}(\theta_{2})
hold

\mu_{1}\in\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G}
and
\mu_{2}\in\llbracket P_{2}\rrbracket_{G}
. By the
rules defining
\operatorname{comp}
, it holds that
\mu_{1}\sim\mu_{2}
and

\mu_{1}\cup\mu_{2}=\mu
. By the semantics of the SPARQL
operator
\operatorname{AND}
, this it holds that
\mu\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}
. Hence,
\theta\in\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}
if and only if
\mu\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}
.

(b)

By definition,

\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu_{1}\in\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G}\\
\mu_{2}\in\llbracket P_{2}\rrbracket_{G}\\
\mu_{1}\sim\mu_{2}\\
\mu=\mu_{1}\cup\mu_{2}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{card}(\mu_{1},\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G})\times\operatorname{card}(\mu_{2},\llbracket P_{2}\rrbracket_{G}).

By the induction hypothesis,

\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})=\hskip-40.00006pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{X_{1}/a_{1},Y/b\}\in\llbracket(p_{1}(X_{1},Y),\Pi_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}\\
\{X_{2}/a_{2},Z/c\}\in\llbracket(p_{2}(X_{2},Y),\Pi_{2})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}\\
\{X_{1}/a_{1},X_{2}/a_{2},X/a\}\in\llbracket(\operatorname{comp}(X_{1},X_{2},X),\Pi)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}\end{subarray}}\hskip-75.0001pt\operatorname{card}(\{X_{1}/a_{1},Y/b\},\llbracket f_{12}(P_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})\times\operatorname{card}(\{X_{2}/a_{2},Z/c\},\llbracket f_{12}(P_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}).

By the semantics of
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
, we conclude that

\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})=\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})
.

We have shown that we can simulate queries of the form
(P_{1}\operatorname{AND}P_{2})
, where
\operatorname{inScope}(P_{1})=\{{{?}{X}},{{?}{Y}}\}
and
\operatorname{inScope}(P_{2})=\{{{?}{X}},{{?}{Z}}\}
, with
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
queries. However, it is not difficult to apply the same argument for queries where
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
have different sets of in-scope variables. Hence, SPARQL queries of the form
(P_{1}\operatorname{AND}P_{2})
are simulable with
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
.

(3)

Let
Q
be a query
(P_{1}\operatorname{EXCEPT}P_{2})
, and
?\bar{X}
be the list of SPARQL variables in set
\operatorname{inScope}(Q)
. The
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
query
f_{12}(Q)
is then
(q(\bar{X}),\Pi)
where
\Pi
is the program that consists of the rules in programs of queries
(p_{1}(\bar{X}),\Pi_{1})=f_{12}(P_{1})
and
(p_{2}(\bar{X}),\Pi_{2})=f_{12}(P_{2})
, and the rule
q(\bar{X})\leftarrow p_{1}(\bar{X}),\neg p_{2}(\bar{X})
.

(a)

By the semantics of
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
,
\theta\in\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}
if and only if
\theta\in\llbracket f_{12}(P_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}
and
\theta\notin\llbracket f_{12}(P_{2})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}
. By the induction hypothesis, the last condition is equivalent to
\mu\in\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G}
and
\mu\notin\llbracket P_{2}\rrbracket_{G}
. By the SPARQL semantics, this is equivalent to
\mu\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}
.

(b)

By definition,
\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G})
and

\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})=\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(P_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}).

By the induction hypothesis,
\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G})=\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(P_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})
. Hence,

\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})=\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}).

Hence, SPARQL queries of the form
(P_{1}\operatorname{EXCEPT}P_{2})
are simulable with
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
.

(4)

Let
Q
be a SPARQL query
(P_{1}\operatorname{UNION}P_{2})
. The
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
query
f_{12}(Q)
is then
(q(\bar{X}),\Pi)
where
\bar{X}
is the list with the variables in set
\operatorname{inScope}(Q)
, and
\Pi
is the program that consists of the rules in program of queries
(p_{1}(\bar{X}),\Pi_{1})=f_{12}(P_{1})
and
(p_{2}(\bar{X}),\Pi_{2})=f_{12}(P_{2})
, and the rules that correspond the operation
\operatorname{UNION}
, namely
q(\bar{X})\leftarrow p_{1}(\bar{X})
and
q(\bar{X})\leftarrow p_{2}(\bar{X})
.

(a)

By the
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
semantics,
\theta
is a solution of
(q(\bar{X}),\Pi)
if and only if
\theta\in\llbracket(p_{1}(\bar{X}),\Pi_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}
or
\theta\in\llbracket(p_{2}(\bar{X}),\Pi_{2})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}
. By the induction hypothesis, this is equivalent to that
\mu\in\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G}
or
\mu\in\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G}
. By the SPARQL semantics, this is equivalent to
\mu\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}
.

(b)

By the
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
semantics,
\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})=\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(P_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})+\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(P_{2})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})
and
\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G})+\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket P_{2}\rrbracket_{G})
.

By the induction hypothesis,
\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(P_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G})
and
\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(P_{2})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket P_{2}\rrbracket_{G})
. Hence
\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})
.

Hence, SPARQL queries of the form
(P_{1}\operatorname{UNION}P_{2})
are simulable with
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
.

(5)

Let
Q
be the SPARQL query
(P\operatorname{FILTER}\varphi)
where is an atomic filter condition (i.e., a filter condition of the form
{{?}{X}}=c
,
{{?}{X}}={{?}{Y}}
, or
\operatorname{bound}({{?}{X}})
), and
L_{\varphi}
be a set of
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
literals defined as follows:

L_{\varphi}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}X=c,\operatorname{bound}(X)&\quad\text{if }\varphi\text{ is }{{?}{X}}=c,\\
X=Y,\operatorname{bound}(X),\operatorname{bound}(Y)&\quad\text{if }\varphi\text{ is }{{?}{X}}={{?}{Y}},\\
\operatorname{bound}(X)&\quad\text{if }\varphi\text{ is }\operatorname{bound}({{?}{X}}).\end{array}\right.

The
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
query
f_{12}(Q)
is then
(q(\bar{X}),\Pi)
where
\bar{X}
is the list with the variables in set
\operatorname{inScope}(Q)
, and
\Pi
is the program that consists of the rules in program of query
(p(\bar{X}),\Pi^{\prime})=f_{12}(P)
, and the rule that corresponds the operation
\operatorname{FILTER}
, namely rule
q(\bar{X})\leftarrow p(\bar{X}),L_{\varphi}
.

(a)

By the
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
semantics,
\theta
is a solution of
(q(\bar{X}),\Pi)
if and only if
\theta\in\llbracket(p(\bar{X}),\Pi^{\prime})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}
, and
\theta(L_{\varphi})\subseteq g_{12}(G)
. By the induction hypothesis,
\theta\in\llbracket(p(\bar{X}),\Pi^{\prime})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}
is equivalent to
\mu\in\llbracket P\rrbracket_{G}
. By construction,
\theta(L_{\varphi})\subseteq\operatorname{atoms}(\Pi^{\prime},g_{12}(G))
if and only if
\mu(\varphi)=\operatorname{\emph{true}}
. By the SPARQL semantics, this is equivalent to
\mu\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}
.

(b)

By construction, every fact in
\theta(L_{\varphi})
occurs once in
g_{12}(G)
. For each fact in
F\in\theta(L_{\varphi})
there is then only one proof that
F\in\operatorname{atoms}(\Pi,g_{12}(G))
. Hence,
\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})=\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(P)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})
. By the induction hypothesis,
\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(P)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket P\rrbracket_{G})
. According to the SPARQL semantics,
\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket P\rrbracket_{G})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})
. Hence,
\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})
.

Hence, SPARQL queries of the form
(P\operatorname{FILTER}\varphi)
are simulable with
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
.

(6)

Let
Q
be the SPARQL query
(\operatorname{SELECT}\bar{X}\;P)
. The
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
query
f_{12}(Q)
is then
(q(\bar{X}),\Pi)
, where
\Pi
is the program that consists of the rules in the program of query
(p(\bar{Y},\Pi^{\prime})=f_{12}(P)
, and the rule that corresponds to the operation projects, namely rule
q(\bar{X})\leftarrow p(\bar{Y}),\operatorname{null}(x_{1}),\dots,\operatorname{null}(x_{n})
, where
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
are the variables that are in
W
but not in
\operatorname{inScope}(P_{1})
.

(a)

By the
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
semantics,
\theta
is a solution of
(q(\bar{X}),\Pi)
if and only if there exists a solution
\theta^{\prime}\in\llbracket(p(\bar{Y},\Pi^{\prime})\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}
such that
\theta(x)=\theta^{\prime}(x)
if
x\in\operatorname{inScope}(Q)\cap\operatorname{inScope}(P)
. Let
\mu=h_{21}(\theta)
and
\mu^{\prime}=h_{21}(\theta^{\prime})
. By construction
\mu=\mu^{\prime}|_{\operatorname{inScope}(Q)}
. By the induction hypothesis,
\mu^{\prime}\in\llbracket P\rrbracket_{G}
. Hence,
\mu\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}
.

(b)

By construction,

\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta^{\prime}|_{\operatorname{inScope}(Q)}=\theta\\
\theta^{\prime}\in\llbracket f_{12}(P)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{card}(\theta^{\prime},\llbracket f_{12}(P)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)}).

By the induction hypothesis,

\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{e1,2}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta^{\prime}|_{\operatorname{inScope}(Q)}=\theta\\
\mu^{\prime}=h_{21}(\theta^{\prime})\\
\mu^{\prime}\in\llbracket P\rrbracket_{G}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{card}(\mu^{\prime},\llbracket P\rrbracket_{G}).

By construction,

\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{e1,2}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu^{\prime}|_{\operatorname{inScope}(Q)}=h_{21}(\theta)\\
\mu^{\prime}\in\llbracket P\rrbracket_{G}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{card}(\mu^{\prime},\llbracket P\rrbracket_{G}).

Hence,
\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{12}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{12}(G)})=\operatorname{card}(h_{21}(\theta),\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})
.

Hence, SPARQL queries of the form
(\operatorname{SELECT}~\bar{X}~P)
are simulable with
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
.

Hence, the triple
(f_{12},g_{12},h_{21})
is a simulation of SPARQL in
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
.
∎

Claim 5 (
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
to SPARQL).

The triple
(f_{21},g_{21},h_{12})
is a simulation of
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
in SPARQL.

Proof.

To prove this claim, we consider only normalized
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
queries
Q
, that is, queries where rules consist of projection rules, join rules and negation rules (see Section
6.2
). This proof follows from induction on the structure of query
Q=(q(\bar{X}),\Pi)
with inductive hypothesis
\theta\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
h_{12}(\theta)\in\llbracket f_{21}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)}
and
\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(h_{12}(\theta),\llbracket f_{21}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)})
.

(1)

If
q
is an extensional predicate, then
f_{21}(Q)
is the SPARQL query

\displaystyle(\operatorname{SELECT}\;{{?}{X}}_{1}\dots{{?}{X}}_{n}\;(({{?}{Y}},\alpha_{0},p)\operatorname{AND}\,({{?}{Y}},\alpha_{1},{{?}{X}}_{1})\operatorname{AND}\cdots\operatorname{AND}\,({{?}{Y}},\alpha_{n},{{?}{X}}_{n})),

where the SPARQL variables
{{?}{X}}_{1}\dots{{?}{X}}_{n}
correspond to the
n

\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
variables in
\bar{X}
.

(a)

By construction,
\theta\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
h_{12}(\theta)\in\llbracket f_{21}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)}
. Indeed, each colored fact
\langle q(a_{1},\dots,a_{n}),i\rangle
in
\operatorname{coloring}(D)
corresponds to a subgraph
\{(u_{i},\alpha_{0},p),(u_{i},\alpha_{1},a_{1}),\cdots,(u_{i},\alpha_{n},a_{n})\}
where
u_{i}
is a fresh IRI to identify the colored fact, and
a_{i}=\theta(x_{i})
, for the
i
-th variable
x_{i}\in\bar{X}
.

(b)

\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{D})
is the cardinality of
q(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})
in multiset
D
. By construction, this is the number of subsgraphs of the form
\{(u_{i},\alpha_{0},p),(u_{i},\alpha_{1},a_{1}),\cdots,(u_{i},\alpha_{n},a_{n})\}
of
g_{21}(D)
. Hence,
\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(h_{12}(\theta),\llbracket f_{21}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)})
.

(2)

If
q
is an intensional predicate, then there are several rules in
\Pi
with head
q(\bar{X})
, each one matching one of the following forms:

•

q(\bar{X})\leftarrow p(\bar{Y})
,

•

q(\bar{X})\leftarrow p_{1}(\bar{Y}_{1}),p_{2}(\bar{Y}_{2})
,

•

q(\bar{X})\leftarrow p_{3}(\bar{Y}_{3}),\neg p_{4}(\bar{Y}_{4})
.

The function
f_{21}(Q)
maps each of these rules to one of the following SPARQL queries:

•

(\operatorname{SELECT}\;\bar{X}\;f_{21}((p(\bar{Y}),\Pi)))
,

•

(f_{21}((p_{1}(\bar{X}),\Pi))\operatorname{AND}f_{21}((p_{2}(\bar{X}),\Pi)))
,

•

(f_{21}((p_{3}(\bar{X}),\Pi))\operatorname{EXCEPT}f_{21}((p_{4}(\bar{X}),\Pi))
.

If
\{R_{1},\dots,R_{n}\}
is the set rules in
\Pi
with predicate
q
in the head, then the SPARQL query
f_{21}(Q)
has the form
(P_{1}\operatorname{UNION}\cdots\operatorname{UNION}P_{n})
, where
P_{i}
is the corresponding SPARQL query for the rule
R_{i}
, for
1\leq i\leq n
.

(a)

First we will prove that the SPARQL query and the
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
query have the same answers. A substitution
\theta
is an answer of query
Q
if and only if at least one of the following conditions holds:

•

For a rule
R_{i}
of the form
q(\bar{X})\leftarrow p(\bar{Y})
, there exists a solution
\theta^{\prime}
of query
(p(\bar{Y}),\Pi)
such that
\theta(x)=\theta^{\prime}(x)
for every variable
x\in\bar{X}
. Then, by the inductive hypothesis, there exists a solution
\mu^{\prime}\in\llbracket f_{21}((p(\bar{Y}),\Pi))\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)}
such that
h_{12}(\mu^{\prime})=\theta^{\prime}
. Let
\mu
be the solution mapping
\mu^{\prime}|_{\bar{X}}
. By construction,
\mu\in\llbracket f_{21}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)}
and
h_{12}(\mu)=\theta
.

•

For a rule
R_{i}
of the form
q(\bar{X})\leftarrow p_{1}(\bar{Y}_{1}),p_{2}(\bar{Y}_{2})
, substitutions
\theta_{1}=\theta|_{\bar{Y}_{1}}
and
\theta_{2}=\theta|_{\bar{Y}_{2}}
are solutions of queries
(p_{1}(\bar{Y}_{1}),\Pi)
and
(p_{2}(\bar{Y}_{2}),\Pi)
. By the inductive hypothesis, there exist two solutions
\mu_{1}\in\llbracket f_{21}((p_{1}(\bar{Y}_{1}),\Pi))\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)}
and
\mu_{2}\in\llbracket f_{21}((p_{2}(\bar{Y}_{2}),\Pi))\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)}
such that
h_{12}(\mu_{1})=\theta_{1}
and
h_{12}(\mu_{2})=\theta_{2}
. Let
\mu
be the solution mapping
\mu_{1}\cup\mu_{2}
. By construction,
\mu\in\llbracket f_{21}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)}
and
h_{12}(\mu)=\theta
.

•

For a rule
R_{i}
of the form
q(\bar{X})\leftarrow p_{3}(\bar{Y}_{3}),\neg p_{4}(\bar{Y}_{4})
, substitution
\theta
is a solution of query
(p_{3}(\bar{Y}_{3}),\Pi)
and
\theta
is not a solution of query
(p_{4}(\bar{Y}_{3}),\Pi)
. By the inductive hypothesis, there exists a solution
\mu\in\llbracket f_{21}((p_{3}(\bar{Y}_{3}),\Pi))\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)}
such that
\mu\notin\llbracket f_{21}((p_{4}(\bar{Y}_{4}),\Pi))\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)}
, and
h_{12}(\mu)=\theta
. By construction,
\mu\in\llbracket f_{21}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)}
.

Hence,
\theta\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if there exists
\mu
such that
f_{12}(\mu)=\theta
and
\mu\in\llbracket f_{21}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)}
.

(b)

We next prove that the answers have the same cardinality in SPARQL and
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
. By definition,

\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{D})=\begin{aligned} &\sum_{\theta^{\prime}|_{\bar{X}}=\theta}\operatorname{card}(\theta^{\prime},\llbracket(p(\bar{Y}),\Pi)\rrbracket_{D})\;+\\
&\operatorname{card}(\theta_{1},\llbracket(p_{1}(\bar{Y}_{1}),\Pi)\rrbracket_{D})\times\operatorname{card}(\theta_{2},\llbracket(p_{2}(\bar{Y}_{2}),\Pi)\rrbracket_{D})\;+\\
&\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket(p_{3}(\bar{Y}_{3}),\Pi)\rrbracket_{D}).\end{aligned}

By the inductive hypothesis,

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{D})

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} &\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta^{\prime}|_{\bar{X}}=\theta\\
h_{12}(\mu^{\prime})=\theta^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{card}(\mu^{\prime},\llbracket f_{21}((p(\bar{Y}),\Pi))\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)})\;+\\
&\operatorname{card}(\mu_{1},\llbracket f_{21}((p_{1}(\bar{Y}_{1}),\Pi))\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)})\times\operatorname{card}(\mu_{2},\llbracket f_{21}((p_{2}(\bar{Y}_{2}),\Pi))\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)})\;+\\
&\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket f_{21}((p_{3}(\bar{Y}_{3}),\Pi))\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)})\end{aligned}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket f_{21}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)}).

Hence, the triple
(f_{21},g_{21},h_{12})
is a simulation of
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
in SPARQL.
∎

Claim 6 (MRA to
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
).

The triple
(f_{32},g_{32},h_{2,3})
is a simulation of MRA in
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
.

Proof.

We prove this claim for normalized MRA expressions where the condition of a selection formula is always an equality atom (e.g.,
\sigma_{A=B}(R)
). This proof follows by induction on the structure of a MRA expression
E
, assuming that given a MRA database
D
, for every subquery
E^{\prime}
of
E
it holds that
t^{\prime}\in\llbracket E^{\prime}\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if there exists a
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
solution
\theta^{\prime}\in\llbracket f_{32}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}
such that
h_{2,3}(\theta^{\prime})=t^{\prime}
.

(1)

If
E
is a relation name
R
then
f_{32}(E)
is the
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
query
(r(\widehat{E}),\emptyset)
where
r
is an extensional predicate.

(a)

By definition,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
t
belongs to the multiset relation corresponding to the relation name
R
in the database
D
. By construction,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
is thus equivalent to
\theta\in\llbracket f_{32}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}
, where
h_{2,3}(\theta)=t
. Indeed,
t\in R^{I}
if and only if
r(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})\in g_{32}(D)
and
t=(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})
.

(b)

The fact that
\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{32}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)})
follows by construction; the cardinality of
t
in the multiset relation corresponding to the relation name
R
is the same as the cardinality of fact
r(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})
in multiset
g_{32}(D)
.

(2)

If
E
is a query
E_{1}\cup E_{2}
, then
\widehat{E}_{1}=\widehat{E}
and
\widehat{E}_{2}=\widehat{E}
, and
f_{32}(E)
is a
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
query
(q(\widehat{E}),\Pi)
such that
f_{32}(E_{1})=(q_{1}(\widehat{E}),\Pi)
and
f_{32}(E_{2})=(q_{2}(\widehat{E}),\Pi)
, and program
\Pi
includes the rules
q(\widehat{E})\leftarrow q_{1}(\widehat{E})
and
q(\widehat{E})\leftarrow q_{2}(\widehat{E})
.

(a)

By definition,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
t\in\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}
or
t\in\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{D}
. By the induction hypothesis,
t\in\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{D}
is equivalent to say that there exists
\theta
such that
h_{2,3}(\theta)=t
and
\theta\in\llbracket f_{32}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}
or
\theta\in\llbracket f_{32}(E_{2})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}
. That is,
\theta\in\llbracket f_{32}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}
.

(b)

Assume the respective answers
t
and
\theta
described in (a).
By definition,

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D})+\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{D}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{32}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)})=\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{32}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)})+\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{32}(E_{2})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}).

By the inductive hypothesis, these two cardinalities are equal.

(3)

If
E
is a query
E_{1}\Join E_{2}
then
\widehat{E}_{1}\cup\widehat{E}_{2}=\widehat{E}
,
f_{32}(E)=(q(\widehat{E}),\Pi)
,
f_{32}(E_{1})=(q_{1}(\widehat{E}_{1}),\Pi)
,
f_{32}(E_{2})=(q_{2}(\widehat{E}_{2}),\Pi)
, and program
\Pi
includes the rule
q(\widehat{E})\leftarrow q_{1}(\widehat{E}_{1}),q_{2}(\widehat{E}_{2})
.

(a)

By definition,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if there exists two tuples
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
such that
t_{1}\sim t_{2}
,
t=t_{1}\cup t_{2}
,
t_{1}\in\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}
and
t_{2}\in\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{D}
. By the induction hypothesis,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if there exists two
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
solutions
\theta_{1}\in\llbracket f_{32}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}
and
\theta_{2}\in\llbracket f_{32}(E_{2})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}
where
h_{2,3}(\theta_{1})=t_{1}
and
h_{2,3}(\theta_{2})=t_{2}
. Let
\theta
be
\theta_{1}\cup\theta_{2}
. By construction,
\theta\in\llbracket f_{32}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}
and
h_{2,3}(\theta)=t
.

(b)

Assume the respective answers
t
,
t_{1}
,
t_{2}
,
\theta
,
\theta_{1}
, and
\theta_{2}
described in (a).
By definition,

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(t_{1},\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D})\times\operatorname{card}(t_{2},\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{32}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)})=\operatorname{card}(\theta_{1},\llbracket f_{32}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)})\times\operatorname{card}(\theta_{2},\llbracket f_{32}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}).

By the inductive hypothesis, these two cardinalities are equal.

(4)

If
E
is a query
E_{1}\setminus E_{2}
then
\widehat{E}_{1}=\widehat{E}
,
\widehat{E}_{1}=\widehat{E}
,
f_{32}(E)=(q(\widehat{E}),\Pi)
,
f_{32}(E_{1})=(q_{1}(\widehat{E}),\Pi)
,
f_{32}(E_{2})=(q_{2}(\widehat{E}),\Pi)
, and program
\Pi
includes the rule
q(\widehat{E})\leftarrow q_{1}(\widehat{E}),\neg q_{2}(\widehat{E})
.

(a)

By definition,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
t\in\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}
and
t\notin\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{D}
. By the induction hypothesis,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if there exists a
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
solution
\theta
such that
\theta\in\llbracket f_{32}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}
,
\theta\notin\llbracket f_{32}(E_{2})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}
, and
h_{2,3}(\theta)=t
. By construction,
\theta\in\llbracket f_{32}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}
.

(b)

Assume the respective answers
t
and
\theta
described in (a). By definition,
\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D})
and
\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{32}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)})=\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{32}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)})
. By the induction hypothesis, these two cardinalities are equal.

(5)

If
E
is a query
\pi_{S}(E_{1})
then
\widehat{E}=S
and
S\subseteq\widehat{E_{1}}
, and
f_{32}(E)
is a
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
query
(q(\bar{X}),\Pi)
such that
f_{32}(E_{1})=(q_{1}(\widehat{E}),\Pi)
, and program
\Pi
includes the rule
q(\widehat{E})\leftarrow q_{1}(\widehat{E}_{1})
.

(a)

By definition,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if there exists a tuple
t_{1}\in\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}
such that
t_{1}|_{\widehat{E}}=t
. By the induction hypothesis,
t_{1}\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if there exists
\theta_{1}\in\llbracket f_{32}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}
such that
h_{2,3}(\theta_{1})=t_{1}
. Let
\theta
be
\theta_{1}|_{\widehat{E}}
. By construction,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
\theta\in\llbracket f_{32}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}
and
h_{2,3}(\theta)=t
.

(b)

Assume the respective answers
t
and
\theta
described in (a). By definition,

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1}\in\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}\\
t_{1}|_{\widehat{E}}=t\end{subarray}}\operatorname{card}(t_{1},\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{32}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta_{1}\in\llbracket f_{32}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}\\
\theta_{1}|_{\widehat{E}}=\theta\end{subarray}}\operatorname{card}(\theta_{1},\llbracket f_{32}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}).

By the induction hypothesis, these two cardinalities are equal.

(6)

If
E
is a query
\rho_{A/B}(E_{1})
then
\widehat{E}=(\widehat{E}_{1}\setminus\{A\})\cup\{B\}
,
f_{32}(E)=(q(\widehat{E}),\Pi)
, and
f_{32}(E_{1})=(q(\widehat{E}_{1}),\Pi)
.

(a)

By definition,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if there exists a tuple
t_{1}\in\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}
where
t(C)=t_{1}(C)
for every attribute
C\in\widehat{E}\setminus\{A\}
, and
t(A)=t_{1}(B)
. By the induction hypothesis,
t_{1}\in\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if there exists a solution
\theta_{1}\in\llbracket f_{32}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}
such that
h_{2,3}(\theta_{1})=t_{1}
. Let
\theta
be the tuple with domain
\widehat{E}
such that
\theta(C)=\theta_{1}(C)
for every attribute
C\in\widehat{E}\setminus\{A\}
, and
\theta(A)=\theta_{1}(B)
. By construction,
\theta\in\llbracket f_{32}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}
if and only if
\theta_{1}\in\llbracket f_{32}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}
and
h_{2,3}(\theta)=t
.

(b)

Assume the respective query answers
t
,
t_{1}
,
\theta
, and
\theta_{1}
described in (a). By definition,

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(t_{1},\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{32}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)})=\operatorname{card}(\theta_{1},\llbracket f_{32}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}).

By the induction hypothesis, these two cardinalities are equal.

(7)

If
E
is a query
\sigma_{A=B}(E_{1})
then
\widehat{E}=\widehat{E}_{1}
,
f_{32}(E)=(q(\widehat{E}),\Pi)
, and
f_{32}(E_{1})=(q_{1}(\widehat{E}_{1}),\Pi)
and program
\Pi
includes the rule
q(\widehat{E})\leftarrow q_{1}(\widehat{E}_{1}),A=B
.

(a)

By definition,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
t\in\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}
and
t(A)=t(B)
. By the induction hypothesis, there is an answer
\theta\in\llbracket f_{32}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}
such that
h_{2,3}(\theta)=t
. By construction,
\theta(A)=\theta(B)
. Then,
\theta\in\llbracket f_{32}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}
.

(b)

Assume the respective query answers
t
and
\theta
described in (a). By definition,

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(t_{1},\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket f_{32}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)})=\operatorname{card}(\theta_{1},\llbracket f_{32}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{32}(D)}).

By the induction hypothesis, these two cardinalities are equal.

Hence, the triple
(f_{32},g_{32},h_{2,3})
is a simulation of MRA in
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
.
∎

Claim 7 (
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
to MRA).

The triple
(f_{2,3},g_{2,3},h_{32})
is a simulation of
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
in SPARQL.

Proof.

To prove this claim we consider only normalized
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
queries
Q
, that is, queries where rules consist of projection rules, join rules and negation rules (see Section
6.2
). This proof follows from induction on the structure of query
Q=(q(\bar{X}),\Pi)
with inductive hypothesis
\theta\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
h_{32}(\theta)\in\llbracket f_{2,3}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)}
and
\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(h_{32}(\theta),\llbracket f_{2,3}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)})
.

(1)

If
q
is a extensional predicate, then
f_{21}(Q)
is the MRA query
\rho_{A_{1}/X_{1}}(\cdots\rho_{A_{n}/X_{n}}(R))
, where the MRA attributes
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
correspond to the
n

\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
variables in
\bar{X}
, and
R
is the relation name corresponding to predicate
q
.

(a)

Let
r
be the MRA relation associated to relation name
R
in the MRA database
g_{2,3}(D)
. Let
\theta
be a
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
answer with domain
\{X_{1},\dots,X_{1}\}
, and
t
be the MRA tuple where
t(A_{i})=\theta(X_{i})
for
1\leq i\leq n
. By definition,
\theta\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
p(\theta(X_{1}),\dots,\theta(X_{n}))\in D
. Because, by definition, each fact
q(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})
in
D
corresponds to a tuple
t\in r
where
t(A_{i})=a_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq n
, then
\theta\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
t\in r
. Let
s
be a MRA tuple with
\hat{s}=\{X_{1},\dots,X_{n}\}
where
s(X_{i})=t(A_{i})
, for
1\leq i\leq n
. By definition,
t\in r
if and only if
s\in\llbracket\rho_{A_{1}/X_{1}}(\cdots\rho_{A_{n}/X_{n}}(R))\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)}
. By construction,
s=h_{32}(\theta)
. Hence,
\theta\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
h_{32}(\theta)\in\llbracket f_{2,3}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)}
.

(b)

The identity
\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(h_{32}(\theta),\llbracket f_{2,3}Q\rrbracket_{f_{2,3}(D)})
follows from the next identities:

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{D})\begin{aligned} &=\operatorname{card}(q(\theta(X_{1}),\dots,\theta(X_{n})),D)\\
&=\operatorname{card}(t,r)\\
&=\operatorname{card}(s,\llbracket f_{2,3}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)})\\
&=\operatorname{card}(h_{32}(\theta),\llbracket f_{2,3}Q\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)}).\end{aligned}

(2)

If
q
is an intensional predicate then there are several rules in
\Pi
with head
q(\bar{X})
, each one has matches of the following forms:

•

q(\bar{X})\leftarrow p(\bar{Y})
,

•

q(\bar{X})\leftarrow p_{1}(\bar{Y}_{1}),p_{2}(\bar{Y}_{2})
,

•

q(\bar{X})\leftarrow p_{3}(\bar{Y}_{3}),\neg p_{4}(\bar{Y}_{4})
.

where
\bar{X}\subseteq\bar{Y}
,
\bar{Y}_{1}\cup\bar{Y}_{2}=\bar{X}
,
\bar{Y}_{3}=\bar{X}
, and
\bar{Y}_{4}=\bar{X}
.
The function
f_{2,3}(Q)
maps each of these rules to one of the following MRA queries:

•

\pi_{\bar{X}}(f_{2,3}((p(\bar{Y}),\Pi)))
,

•

(f_{2,3}((p_{1}(\bar{X}),\Pi))\Join f_{2,3}((p_{2}(\bar{X}),\Pi)))
,

•

(f_{2,3}((p_{3}(\bar{X}),\Pi))\setminus f_{2,3}((p_{4}(\bar{X}),\Pi)))
.

If
\{R_{1},\dots,R_{n}\}
is the set rules in
\Pi
with predicate
q
in the head, then the MRA query
f_{2,3}(Q)
has the form
(E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{n})
, where
E_{i}
is the corresponding MRA expression for the rule
R_{i}
, for
1\leq i\leq n
.

(a)

First, we will prove that the MRA expression and the
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
query have the same answers. A substitution
\theta
is an answer of query
Q
if and only if one of the following conditions holds:

•

There exists a solution
\theta^{\prime}
of query
(p(\bar{Y}),\Pi)
such that
\theta(x)=\theta^{\prime}(x)
for every variable
x\in\bar{X}
. By the induction hypothesis, there exists a solution
t^{\prime}\in\llbracket f_{2,3}((p(\bar{Y}),\Pi))\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)}
such that
h_{32}(t^{\prime})=\theta^{\prime}
. Let
t
be the solution mapping
t^{\prime}|_{\bar{X}}
. By construction,
t\in\llbracket f_{2,3}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)}
and
h_{32}(t)=\theta
.

•

Substitutions
\theta_{1}=\theta|_{\bar{Y}_{1}}
and
\theta_{2}=\theta|_{\bar{Y}_{2}}
are solutions of queries
(p_{1}(\bar{Y}_{1}),\Pi)
and
(p_{2}(\bar{Y}_{2}),\Pi)
. By the induction hypothesis, there exists two solutions
t_{1}\in\llbracket f_{2,3}((p_{1}(\bar{Y}_{1}),\Pi))\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)}
and
t_{2}\in\llbracket f_{2,3}((p_{2}(\bar{Y}_{2}),\Pi))\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)}
such that
h_{32}(t_{1})=\theta_{1}
and
h_{32}(t_{2})=\theta_{2}
. Let
t
be the MRA solution
t_{1}\cup t_{2}
. By construction,
t\in\llbracket f_{2,3}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)}
and
h_{32}(t)=\theta
.

•

\theta
is a solution of query
(p_{3}(\bar{Y}_{3}),\Pi)
and
\theta
is not a solution of query
(p_{4}(\bar{Y}_{3}),\Pi)
. By the induction hypothesis, there exists a solution
t\in\llbracket f_{2,3}((p_{3}(\bar{Y}_{3}),\Pi))\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)}
such that
t\notin\llbracket f_{2,3}((p_{4}(\bar{Y}_{4}),\Pi))\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)}
, and
h_{32}(t)=\theta
. By construction,
t\in\llbracket f_{2,3}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)}
.

Hence,
\theta\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if there exists
\mu
such that
f_{12}(\mu)=\theta
and
\mu\in\llbracket f_{21}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{21}(D)}
.

(b)

We next prove that the answers have the same cardinality in MRA and
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
.
By definition,

\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{D})=\begin{aligned} &\sum_{\theta^{\prime}|_{\bar{X}}=\theta}\operatorname{card}(\theta^{\prime},\llbracket(p(\bar{Y}),\Pi)\rrbracket_{D})\;+\\
&\operatorname{card}(\theta_{1},\llbracket(p_{1}(\bar{Y}_{1}),\Pi)\rrbracket_{D})\times\operatorname{card}(\theta_{2},\llbracket(p_{2}(\bar{Y}_{2}),\Pi)\rrbracket_{D})\;+\\
&\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket(p_{3}(\bar{Y}_{3}),\Pi)\rrbracket_{D}).\end{aligned}

By the induction hypothesis,

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\theta,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{D})

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} &\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta^{\prime}|_{\bar{X}}=\theta\\
h_{12}(t^{\prime})=\theta^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{card}(t^{\prime},\llbracket f_{2,3}((p(\bar{Y}),\Pi))\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)})\;+\\
&\operatorname{card}(t_{1},\llbracket f_{2,3}((p_{1}(\bar{Y}_{1}),\Pi))\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)})\times\operatorname{card}(t_{2},\llbracket f_{2,3}((p_{2}(\bar{Y}_{2}),\Pi))\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)})\;+\\
&\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{2,3}((p_{3}(\bar{Y}_{3}),\Pi))\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)})\end{aligned}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{2,3}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{2,3}(D)}).

Hence, the triple
(f_{2,3},g_{2,3},h_{32})
is a simulation of
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
in MRA.
∎

Claim 8 (MRA to SPARQL).

The triple
(f_{31},g_{31},h_{13})
is a simulation of
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
in SPARQL.

Proof.

We proof this claim for normalized MRA expressions where the condition of a selection formula is always an equality atom (e.g.,
\sigma_{A=B}(R)
). We proof this claim by induction on the structure of a MRA expression
E
, assuming that given an MRA database
D
, for every subquery
E^{\prime}
of
E
it holds that
t^{\prime}\in\llbracket E^{\prime}\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if there exists a SPARQL solution
\mu^{\prime}\in\llbracket f_{31}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
such that
h_{13}(\mu^{\prime})=t^{\prime}
.

(1)

If
E
is a relation name
R
then
f_{31}(E)
is the SPARQL query
(\operatorname{SELECT}\;{?A_{1}}\cdots{?A_{n}}\;P)
where
P
is the basic graph pattern

(({{?}{X}},u_{b},u_{r})\operatorname{AND}({{?}{X}},u_{1},?A_{1})\operatorname{AND}\cdots\operatorname{AND}({{?}{X}},u_{n},?A_{n})))
, and
?A_{1},\dots,?A_{n}
are the variables corresponding to the attributes associated to relation name
R
.

(a)

Let
t
be an MRA tuple with
\hat{t}=\widehat{R}
, and
\mu
be an SPARQL mapping with
h_{13}(\mu)=t
. By definition,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if tuple
t
belongs to multiset relation
R^{D}
. By construction,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
is thus equivalent to the existence of an an IRI
u
such that the triples
(u,u_{b},u_{r})
,
(u,u_{1},t(A_{1}))
, …,
(u,u_{n},t(A_{n}))
belong to the RDF graph
g_{31}(D)
. By definition, there exists such an IRI
u
if and only if there exists a SPARQL mapping
\mu^{\prime}\in\llbracket P\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
where
\mu^{\prime}({{?}{X}})=u
and
\mu(?A_{i})=t(a_{i})
, for
1\leq i\leq n
. By construction,
\mu^{\prime}|_{?A_{1},\dots,?A_{n}}=\mu
. Then,
\mu^{\prime}\in\llbracket P\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
if and only if
\mu\in\llbracket f_{31}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
.

(b)

The fact that
\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket f_{31}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)})
follows by construction; the cardinality of
t
in the multiset relation
R^{D}
is the same as the number of IRIs
u
such that such that the triples
(u,u_{b},u_{r})
,
(u,u_{1},t(A_{1}))
, …,
(u,u_{n},t(A_{n}))
belong to the RDF graph
g_{31}(D)
.

(2)

If
E
is a query
E_{1}\cup E_{2}
, then
\widehat{E}_{1}=\widehat{E}
,
\widehat{E}_{2}=\widehat{E}
, and
f_{31}(E)
is the SPARQL query
(f_{31}(E_{1})\operatorname{UNION}f_{31}(E_{2}))
.

(a)

Let
t
be an MRA tuple with
\hat{t}=\widehat{E}
, and
\mu
be an SPARQL mapping such that
h_{13}(\mu)=t
. By definition,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
t\in\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}
or
t\in\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{D}
. By the induction hypothesis,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
\mu\in\llbracket f_{31}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
or
\mu\in\llbracket f_{31}(E_{2})\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
. By definition,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
\mu\in\llbracket f_{31}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
.

(b)

Assume the respective answers
t
and
\mu
described in (a).
By definition,

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D})+\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{D}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket f_{31}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket f_{31}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)})+\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket f_{31}(E_{2})\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}).

By the induction hypothesis, these two cardinalities are equal.

(3)

If
E
is a query
E_{1}\Join E_{2}
then
\widehat{E}_{1}\cup\widehat{E}_{2}=\widehat{E}
, and
f_{31}(E)
is the SPARQL query
(f_{31}(E_{1})\operatorname{AND}f_{31}(E_{2}))
.

(a)

Let
t
be an MRA tuple with
\hat{t}=\widehat{E}
, and
\mu
be an SPARQL mapping such that
h_{13}(\mu)=t
. By definition,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if there exists two tuples
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
such that
t_{1}\sim t_{2}
,
t=t_{1}\cup t_{2}
,
t_{1}\in\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}
and
t_{2}\in\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{D}
. By the induction hypothesis,
t_{1}\in\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}
and
t_{2}\in\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{D}
if and only there exist two SPARQL mappings
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
such that
h_{13}(\mu_{1})=t_{1}
,
h_{13}(\mu_{2})=t_{2}
,
\mu_{1}\in\llbracket f_{31}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
, and
\mu_{1}\in\llbracket f_{31}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
. By construction,
\mu_{1}\sim\mu_{2}
,
\mu_{1}\cup\mu_{2}=\mu
, and
\mu\in\llbracket f_{31}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
. Hence,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
\mu\in\llbracket f_{31}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
.

(b)

Assume the respective answers
t
,
t_{1}
,
t_{2}
,
\mu
,
\mu_{1}
, and
\mu_{2}
described in (a).
By definition,

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(t_{1},\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D})\times\operatorname{card}(t_{2},\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket f_{31}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)})=\operatorname{card}(\mu_{1},\llbracket f_{31}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)})\times\operatorname{card}(\mu_{2},\llbracket f_{31}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}).

By the induction hypothesis, these two cardinalities are equal.

(4)

If
E
is a query
E_{1}\setminus E_{2}
then
\widehat{E}_{1}=\widehat{E}
,
\widehat{E}_{1}=\widehat{E}
, and
f_{31}(E)
is the SPARQL query
(f_{31}(E_{1})\operatorname{EXCEPT}f_{31}(E_{2}))
.

(a)

Let
t
be an MRA tuple with
\hat{t}=\widehat{E}
, and
\mu
be an SPARQL mapping such that
h_{13}(\mu)=t
. By definition,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
t\in\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}
and
t\notin\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{D}
. By the induction hypothesis,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
\mu\in\llbracket f_{31}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
and
\mu\notin\llbracket f_{31}(E_{2})\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
. Hence,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
\mu\in\llbracket f_{31}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
.

(b)

Assume the respective answers
t
and
\mu
described in (a). By definition,
\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D})
and
\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket f_{31}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket f_{31}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)})
. By the induction hypothesis, these two cardinalities are equal.

(5)

If
E
is a query
\pi_{S}(E_{1})
then
\widehat{E}=S
and
S\subseteq\widehat{E_{1}}
, and
f_{31}(E)
is a SPARQL query
(\operatorname{SELECT}\;W\;f_{31}(E_{1}))
such that
W
is the corresponding set of SPARQL variables for the set of attributes
S
.

(a)

Let
t
be an MRA tuple with
\hat{t}=\widehat{E}
. By definition,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if there exists a tuple
t_{1}\in\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}
such that
t_{1}|_{\widehat{E}}=t
. By the induction hypothesis,
t_{1}\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if there exists
\mu_{1}\in\llbracket f_{31}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
such that
h_{13}(\mu_{1})=t_{1}
. Let
\mu
be
\mu_{1}|_{W}
. By construction,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
\mu\in\llbracket f_{31}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
and
h_{13}(\mu)=t
.

(b)

Assume the respective answers
t
and
\mu
described in (a). By definition,

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1}\in\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}\\
t_{1}|_{\widehat{E}}=t\end{subarray}}\operatorname{card}(t_{1},\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket f_{31}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu_{1}\in\llbracket f_{31}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}\\
\mu_{1}|_{W}=\mu\end{subarray}}\operatorname{card}(\mu_{1},\llbracket f_{31}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}).

By the induction hypothesis, these two cardinalities are equal.

(6)

If
E
is a query
\rho_{A/B}(E_{1})
then
\widehat{E}=(\widehat{E}_{1}\setminus\{A\})\cup\{B\}
,
f_{31}(E)
is the SPARQL query that results from consistently renaming variable
?A
as variable
?B
in
f_{31}(E_{1})
(i.e.,
\operatorname{subs}_{?A/?B}(A)
), and
?A
and
?B
are the corresponding SPARQL variables for atributes
A
and
B
.

(a)

By definition,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if there exists a tuple
t_{1}\in\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}
where
t(C)=t_{1}(C)
for every attribute
C\in\widehat{E}\setminus\{A\}
, and
t(A)=t_{1}(B)
. By the induction hypothesis,
t_{1}\in\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if there exists a solution
\mu_{1}\in\llbracket f_{31}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
such that
h_{13}(\mu_{1})=t_{1}
. Let
\mu
be the SPARQL mapping with domain
(\operatorname{dom}(\mu^{\prime})\setminus\{?A\})\cup\{?B\}
such that
\mu(?C)=\mu_{1}(?C)
for every variable
?C\in\operatorname{dom}(\mu^{\prime})\setminus\{?A\}
, and
\mu(?A)=\mu_{1}(?B)
. By construction,
h_{13}(\mu)=t
. Hence,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
\mu\in\llbracket f_{31}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
.

(b)

Assume the respective query answers
t
,
t_{1}
,
\mu
, and
\mu_{1}
described in (a). By definition,

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(t_{1},\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket f_{31}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)})=\operatorname{card}(\mu_{1},\llbracket f_{31}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}).

By the induction hypothesis, these two cardinalities are equal.

(7)

If
E
is a query
\sigma_{A=B}(E_{1})
then
\widehat{E}=\widehat{E}_{1}
,
f_{31}(E)=(q(\widehat{E}),\Pi)
, and
f_{31}(E_{1})
is the SPARQ query
(P_{1}\;\operatorname{FILTER}\;{?A}={?B})
where
?A
and
?B
are the corresponding SPARQL variables for the MRA attributes
A
and
B
.

(a)

By definition,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
t\in\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}
and
t(A)=t(B)
. By the induction hypothesis, there is an answer
\mu\in\llbracket f_{31}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
such that
h_{13}(\mu)=t
. By construction,
\mu(A)=\mu(B)
. Then,
t\in\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
\mu\in\llbracket f_{31}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}
.

(b)

Assume the respective query answers
t
and
\theta
described in (a). By definition,

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket E\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(t_{1},\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{D}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket f_{31}(E)\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)})=\operatorname{card}(\mu_{1},\llbracket f_{31}(E_{1})\rrbracket_{g_{31}(D)}).

By the induction hypothesis, these two cardinalities are equal.

Hence, the triple
(f_{31},g_{31},h_{13})
is a simulation of MRA in
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
.
∎

Claim 9 (SPARQL to MRA).

The triple
(f_{13},g_{13},h_{31})
is a simulation of
\text{NRMD}^{\neg}
in SPARQL.

Proof.

To prove this claim we show that, for every SPARQL query
Q
and RDF graph
G
, it holds that
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}=h_{31}(\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{13}})
. For simplicity, we write
D
instead of
D
. We next show this identity by induction on the structure of a normalized SPARQL query
Q
. In this proof we assume that
t
is a MRA tuple with the attributes of the MRA expression
f_{13}(Q)
, and
\mu
is the SPARQL mapping
h_{31}(t)
. To show that
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}=h_{31}(\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{13}})
, we prove that
\mu\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}
if and only if
t\in\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D}
and
\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})=\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D})
.

(1)

Case
Q
is a triple pattern.

(a)

By definition, every triple pattern is translated to a MRA expression consisting of operations
\sigma
,
\rho
, and
\pi
over the relation name
\operatorname{Trip}
. For example, if
Q
is the triple pattern
({{?}{X}},p,{{?}{X}})
, then
f_{12}(Q)
is the expression
\Pi_{X}(\rho_{S/X}(\sigma_{P=p\land S=O}(\operatorname{Trip})))
. It can be shown that the triple pattern
Q=({{?}{X}},p,{{?}{X}})
is equivalent to the SPARQL query
Q^{\prime}=(\operatorname{SELECT}\;{{?}{X}}\;(({{?}{X}},?P,?O)\operatorname{FILTER}(?P=p\,\land\,{{?}{X}}=?O))
. Then,
\mu\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}
if and only if there exists a solution
\mu^{\prime}\in\llbracket({{?}{X}},?P,?O)\rrbracket_{G}
such that
\mu=\mu^{\prime}|_{\{X\}}
,
\mu^{\prime}(?P)=p
and
\mu^{\prime}({{?}{X}})=\mu^{\prime}(?O)
. Without loss of generality, let
\mu^{\prime}({{?}{X}})=a
. Such mapping
\mu^{\prime}
is a solution of the triple pattern
({{?}{X}},?P,?O)
if and only if
(a,p,a)\in G
. By construction,
(a,p,a)\in G
if and only if
(a,p,a)\in\operatorname{Trip}^{D}
, where
D
is the MRA database
D
. If
(a,p,a)\in\operatorname{Trip}^{D}
then
t\in\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D}
. Hence,
\mu\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}
if and only if
t\in\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D}
. So far, we showed that the claim follows for a particular triple pattern. This result can be extended for all the triple patterns following the same procedure.

(b)

By construction,
\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket f_{1,1}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{1,1}(G)})=1
and
\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})=1
. Hence,
\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{1,1}(Q)\rrbracket_{g_{1,1}(G)})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})
.

(2)

Case
Q
is a query
(P_{1}\operatorname{AND}P_{2})
. Without loss of generality assume that
\operatorname{inScope}(P_{1})=\{{{?}{X}},{{?}{Y}}\}
and
\operatorname{inScope}(P_{2})=\{{{?}{X}},{{?}{Z}}\}
. By definition, the MRA expression for query
Q
is:

\displaystyle f_{13}(Q)

\displaystyle=f_{13}(P_{1})*f_{13}(P_{2})

\displaystyle=\pi_{X,Y,Z}(\rho_{A_{1}/X_{1}}(\rho_{A_{2}/X_{2}}(\rho_{A/X}(\operatorname{Comp})))\Join\rho_{X/X_{1}}(f_{13}(P_{1}))\Join\rho_{X/X_{2}}(f_{13}(P_{2}))).

(a)

If
t\in\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D}
then, there are MRA tuples
t_{1}\in\llbracket f_{13}(P_{1})\rrbracket_{D}
,
t_{2}\in\llbracket f_{13}(P_{2})\rrbracket_{D}
, and
t_{3}\in\llbracket\operatorname{Comp}\rrbracket_{D}
such that
t(X)=t_{3}(A)
,
t(Y)=t_{2}(Y)
,
t(Z)=t_{3}(Z)
, and
t_{1}(X)=t_{3}(A_{1})
,
t_{2}(X)=t_{3}(A_{2})
. Let
\mu_{1}=h_{31}(t_{1})
,
\mu_{2}=h_{31}(t_{2})
, and
\mu_{3}=h_{31}(t_{3})
. By the induction hypothesis in
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
,
t_{1}\in\llbracket f_{13}(P_{1})\rrbracket_{D}
and
t_{2}\in\llbracket f_{13}(P_{2})\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
\mu_{1}\in\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G}
and
\mu_{2}\in\llbracket P_{2}\rrbracket_{G}
. By the definition of
\operatorname{Comp}^{D}
, it holds that
\mu_{1}\sim\mu_{2}
and

\mu_{1}\cup\mu_{2}=\mu
. By the semantics of the SPARQL
operator
\operatorname{AND}
, it holds then that
\mu\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}
. Hence,
t\in\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
\mu\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}
.

(b)

By definition,

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu_{1}\in\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G}\\
\mu_{2}\in\llbracket P_{2}\rrbracket_{G}\\
\mu_{1}\sim\mu_{2}\\
\mu=\mu_{1}\cup\mu_{2}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{card}(\mu_{1},\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G})\times\operatorname{card}(\mu_{2},\llbracket P_{2}\rrbracket_{G}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1}\in\llbracket f_{13}(P_{1})\rrbracket_{D}\\
t_{2}\in\llbracket f_{13}(P_{2})\rrbracket_{D}\\
t_{3}\in\llbracket\operatorname{Comp}\rrbracket_{D}\\
\varphi(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3})\end{subarray}}\operatorname{card}(t_{3},\llbracket\operatorname{Comp}\rrbracket_{D})\times\operatorname{card}(t_{1},\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{D})\times\operatorname{card}(t_{2},\llbracket P_{2}\rrbracket_{D}),

where
\varphi(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3})
is a condition coresponding to the compatibility, that is true if and only if the following statements hold:

(i)

t_{1}(Y)=t(Y)
,

(ii)

t_{2}(Z)=t(Z)
, and

(iii)

either

(A)

(t_{1}(X)=t(X)
and
t_{2}(X)=t(X)
,

(B)

(t_{1}(X)=t(X)
and
t_{2}(X)=t(X)
, or

(C)

(t_{1}(X)=t(X)
and
t_{2}(X)=t(X)
.

By the induction hypothesis,
\operatorname{card}(\mu_{1},\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G})=\operatorname{card}(t_{1},\llbracket f_{13}(P_{1})\rrbracket_{D})
and
\operatorname{card}(\mu_{2},\llbracket P_{2}\rrbracket_{G})=\operatorname{card}(t_{2},\llbracket f_{13}(P_{2})\rrbracket_{D})
. By construction,
\operatorname{card}(t_{3},\llbracket\operatorname{Comp}\rrbracket_{D})=1
. Hence,
\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})=\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D})
.

(3)

Case
Q
is a query
(P_{1}\operatorname{EXCEPT}P_{2})
. Let
?\bar{X}
be the list of SPARQL variables in set
\operatorname{inScope}(Q)
. The MRA query
f_{13}(Q)
is then
f_{13}(P_{1})\setminus f_{13}(P_{2})
.

(a)

By definition,
t\in\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
t\in\llbracket f_{13}(P_{1})\rrbracket_{D}
and
t\notin\llbracket f_{13}(P_{2})\rrbracket_{D}
. By the induction hypothesis, the last condition is equivalent to
\mu\in\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G}
and
\mu\notin\llbracket P_{2}\rrbracket_{G}
. By the SPARQL semantics,
t\in\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{G}
if and only if
\mu\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}
.

(b)

By definition,
\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G})
and
\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(P_{1})\rrbracket_{D})
. By the induction hypothesis,
\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G})=\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(P_{1})\rrbracket_{D})
. Hence,
\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})=\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D})
.

(4)

Case
Q
is a SPARQL query
(P_{1}\operatorname{UNION}P_{2})
. The MRA expression
f_{13}(Q)
is then
f_{13}(P_{1})\cup f_{13}(P_{2})
.

(a)

By definition,
t\in\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{G}
if and only if
t\in\llbracket f_{13}(P_{1})\rrbracket_{D}
or
t\in\llbracket f_{13}(P_{2})\rrbracket_{D}
. By the induction hypothesis,
t\in\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{G}
if and only if
\mu\in\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G}
or
\mu\in\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G}
. By the SPARQL semantics,
t\in\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{G}
if and only if
\mu\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}
.

(b)

By definition,

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(P_{1})\rrbracket_{D})+\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(P_{2})\rrbracket_{D}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G})+\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket P_{2}\rrbracket_{G}).

By the induction hypothesis,

\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(P_{1})\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket P_{1}\rrbracket_{G})\quad\text{and}\quad\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(P_{2})\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket P_{2}\rrbracket_{G}).

Hence
\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})
.

(5)

Case
Q
is a SPARQL query
(P\operatorname{FILTER}\varphi)
where
\varphi
is an atomic filter condition (i.e., a filter condition of the form
{{?}{X}}=c
,
{{?}{X}}={{?}{Y}}
, or
\operatorname{bound}({{?}{X}})
). The MRA expression
f_{13}(Q)
is then
\sigma_{\psi}(f_{13}(P))
where
\psi
is the MRA selection condition defined as follows:

\psi=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}X=c\land\neg(X=\bot)&\quad\text{if }\varphi\text{ is }{{?}{X}}=c,\\
X=Y\land\neg(X=\bot)\land\neg(Y=\bot)&\quad\text{if }\varphi\text{ is }{{?}{X}}={{?}{Y}},\\
\neg(X=\bot)&\quad\text{if }\varphi\text{ is }\operatorname{bound}({{?}{X}}).\end{array}\right.

(a)

By definition,
t\in\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
t\in\llbracket f_{13}(P)\rrbracket_{D}
and
t
satisfies condition
\psi
. It is not difficult to see that
t
satisfies condition
\psi
if and only if
\mu
satisfies condition
\varphi
. By the induction hypothesis,
t\in\llbracket f_{13}(P)\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
\mu\in\llbracket P\rrbracket_{G}
. Hence,
\mu\in\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
\mu\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}
.

(b)

By definition, if
t
and
\mu
satisfy the respective conditions, then:

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(P)\rrbracket_{D}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket P\rrbracket_{G}).

By the induction hypothesis,
\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(P)\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket P\rrbracket_{G})
. Hence,

\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}).

(6)

Case
Q
is a SPARQL query
(\operatorname{SELECT}\;?\bar{X}\;P)
. The MRA expression
f_{13}(Q)
is then
\pi_{\bar{X}}(f_{13}(P)\Join\Delta_{\bar{Y}})
, where
\bar{X}
is the corresponding set of attributes for the variables
?\bar{X}
and
\bar{Y}
is the correponding set of attributes for the variables in set
\operatorname{inScope}(P)\setminus\operatorname{inScope}(Q)
.

(a)

By definition,
t\in\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
t(Y)=\bot
for every attribute name
Y\in\bar{Y}
and there exists a solution
t^{\prime}\in\llbracket f_{13}(P)\rrbracket_{D}
such that
t^{\prime}(A)=t(A)
for every attribute
A\in\bar{X}\setminus\bar{Y}
. Let
\mu^{\prime}=h_{31}(t^{\prime})
. By the induction hypothesis,
t^{\prime}\in\llbracket f_{13}(P)\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
\mu^{\prime}\in\llbracket P\rrbracket_{G}
. By construction
\mu=\mu^{\prime}|_{?\bar{X}}
. Hence,
t\in\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D}
if and only if
t\in\llbracket P\rrbracket_{G}
.

(b)

By construction,

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t^{\prime}|_{\operatorname{inScope}(Q)}=t\\
t^{\prime}\in\llbracket f_{13}(P)\rrbracket_{D}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{card}(t^{\prime},\llbracket f_{13}(P)\rrbracket_{D}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\mu,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu^{\prime}|_{\operatorname{inScope}(Q)}=\mu\\
\mu^{\prime}\in\llbracket P\rrbracket_{G}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{card}(\mu^{\prime},\llbracket P\rrbracket_{G}),

By the induction hypothesis,
\operatorname{card}(t^{\prime},\llbracket f_{13}(P)\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(\mu^{\prime},\llbracket P\rrbracket_{G})
. Hence,

\operatorname{card}(t,\llbracket f_{13}(Q)\rrbracket_{D})=\operatorname{card}(\mu^{\prime},\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{G}).

Hence, the triple
(f_{13},g_{13},h_{31})
is a simulation of SPARQL in MRA.
∎
